stty: 'standard input': Inappropriate ioctl for device

ProSE -- Annotates bottom-up MS/MS spectra using ProSE.
Full documentation: http://www.openms.de/doxygen/nightly/html/TOPP_ProSE.html
Version: 3.7.0-pre-HEAD-2026-11-04 Nov  4 2026, 21:27:12, Revision: d60780f
To cite OpenMS:
 + Pfeuffer, J., Bielow, C., Wein, S. et al.. OpenMS 3 enables reproducible analysis of large-scale mass spectrometry data. Nat Methods (2024). doi:10.1038/s41592-024-02197-7.

Usage:
  ProSE <options>

Options (mandatory options marked with '*'):
  -in <files>*                                              Input spectrum file(s). Multiple files are searched against the same database; the fragment index is built once and reused. (valid formats: 'mzML', 'mzpeak')
  -database <file>*                                         Input protein sequence database in FASTA format. (valid formats: 'fasta', 'fa', 'faa')
  -out <files>                                              Output identification file(s), one per -in: idXML or an idparquet directory bundle (format by extension). Must have the same number of entries as -in. (valid formats: 'idXML', 'idparquet')
  -out_idxml <files>                                        Deprecated alias of -out (same behaviour, idXML or idparquet by extension). Cannot be combined with -out. (valid formats: 'idXML', 'idparquet')
  -out_qpx <dir>                                            Output directory for QPX exchange format Parquet files. Writes per-input runs/<basename>.psm.parquet and runs/<basename>.pg.parquet, and quantms.psm.parquet and quantms.pg.parquet of the whole run (see also -qpx_extended): the folder is one QPX dataset (prefix quantms). The pg files are written only when protein groups were actually inferred, which needs 'FDR:protein' > 0 (with decoys), or '-out_merged' with more than one input; an identification-only run produces no pg file rather than an empty one. The Parquet files of an earlier run in the folder (quantms.*.parquet, runs/*.parquet) are removed.
  -out_parquet <dir>                                        Output directory for OpenMS internal format Parquet files. Writes per-input runs/<basename>.psm/proteins/pg/search_params.parquet, and openms.psm/proteins/pg/search_params.parquet of the whole run: the folder is one dataset (prefix openms). With several inputs and 'FDR:protein' > 0 or -out_merged also merged.proteins.parquet and merged.pg.parquet (the pooled proteins). The Parquet files of an earlier run in the folder (openms.*.parquet, merged.*.parquet, runs/*.parquet) are removed.
  -out_merged <file>                                        Optional output of all PSMs pooled across the input files, with a protein inference across them (BasicProteinInferenceAlgorithm) and, with 'Search:FDR:protein' > 0, the picked-protein FDR (see the tool documentation, 'Final results vs. intermediates'). Only useful with multiple -in files. idXML or an idparquet directory bundle (format by extension). (valid formats: 'idXML', 'idparquet')
  -out_pin <files>                                          Output Percolator input (.pin/.tsv) file(s) for external rescoring. Must have the same number of entries as -in. It holds the PSMs before the rescoring and before any FDR filter, with ProSE's own scores and all reported hits: 'Search:rescore' and 'Search:FDR:*' apply to the other outputs only. (valid formats: 'tsv')
  -out_mod_analysis_dir <dir>                               Optional directory to write modification-analysis tables (delta-mass, PTM stats) when running in open-search mode. When set, per-file tables are written using each input file's basename and an additional aggregate table is written across all input files. The analysis covers the PSMs of each input that pass 'Search:FDR:PSM', before the protein FDR (the evidence for the mass shifts, also of PSMs whose proteins the protein FDR removes). Has no effect in closed-search mode.
  -summary_out <file>                                       Optional YAML file capturing the end-of-search report (per-file identification statistics, shared configuration/database/index facts, timing and output manifest) for pipeline ingestion. The same information is printed to the command line unless -no_summary is set. (valid formats: 'yaml')
  -no_summary                                               Suppress the end-of-search report printed to the command line. The -summary_out YAML file (if requested) is still written.
  -qpx_extended                                             With -out_qpx: add optional columns to the QPX psm view (all scores in 'additional_scores', 'spectrum_reference', 'isotope_error', 'protein_mappings'; in the per-input files also PeptDeep's predicted b/y intensities) and write the QPX mz view of the spectra of each input's PSMs (filtered peaks, see -qpx_peak_top) and the QPX run, sample and provenance views (quantms.run/sample/provenance.parquet: the inputs with their statistics, the steps of the search with their parameters); a run without protein inference gets one protein group per protein. See the tool documentation.
  -qpx_peak_window <Th>                                     -qpx_extended: the m/z window of the peak filter of the mz view. (default: '100.0') (min: '1.0')
  -qpx_peak_top <n>                                         -qpx_extended: the most intense peaks kept per -qpx_peak_window (with every annotated peak); 0 keeps all peaks. (default: '100') (min: '0')
  -keep_decoys                                              Keep the decoy PSMs and proteins that pass the FDR thresholds in the outputs of a final result (Search:FDR:protein > 0; see the tool documentation, 'Final results vs. intermediates').

Search:
  -Search:enzyme <choice>                                   The enzyme used for peptide digestion. (default: 'Trypsin') (valid: 'Chymotrypsin/P', 'CNBr', 'Formic_acid', 'Lys-C', 'Lys-N', 'Lys-C/P', 'PepsinA', 'TrypChymo', 'Trypsin/P', 'V8-DE', 'V8-E', 'leukocyte elastase', 'proline endopeptidase', 'Trypsin', 'glutamyl endopeptidase', 'Alpha-lytic protease', '2-iodobenzoate', 'iodosobenzoate', 'staphylococcal protease/D', 'proline-endopeptidase/HKR', 'Glu-C+P', 'PepsinA + P', 'cyanogen-bromide', 'Clostripain/P', 'elastase-trypsin-chymotrypsin', 'no cleavage', 'unspecific cleavage', 'Arg-C', 'Arg-C/P', 'Asp-N', 'Asp-N/B', 'Asp-N_ambic', 'Chymotrypsin')
  -Search:decoys <choice>                                   Decoy handling for target-decoy FDR. 'auto': ensure decoys are available — reuse decoys already present in the database (marker auto-detected, prefix or suffix) or generate them if none are found. Supplied decoys are reused only at 0.5 to 2 decoys per target protein: target-decoy FDR needs about one decoy per target, so a database with far fewer or more is refused (use 'generate'). 'generate': always (re)generate decoys from the target proteins (any pre-existing decoys are removed first to avoid decoy-of-decoy entries). 'ignore': search the target proteins only — any decoys present in the database are removed. (default: 'generate') (valid: 'auto', 'generate', 'ignore')
  -Search:decoy_prefix <text>                               Accession prefix prepended to decoy proteins when ProSE generates them (modes 'auto' without existing decoys, and 'generate'). Pre-existing decoys in the database are detected automatically (any common marker, used as prefix or suffix) and do not require this setting. (default: 'DECOY_')
  -Search:decoy_method <choice>                             How ProSE generates decoys (decoys 'generate', or 'auto' with a database without decoys). 'reverse' (ProSEAlgorithm's default; the ProSE tool's default is 'keep_termini'): every enzymatic segment of the protein is reversed except its last residue (the last segment entirely), an initial Met stays first with peptide:clip_nterm_methionine. 'keep_termini': the construction of OpenMS DecoyDatabase (method reverse, Decoy:keepPeptideNTerm/keepPeptideCTerm true) with the search enzyme and Decoy:non_shuffle_pattern 'KRP': in every segment of a digest without missed cleavages, the first and the last residue and every K, R and P stay in place and the rest is reversed. For enzymes whose cleavage sites are fixed by these residues (Trypsin, Trypsin/P, Lys-C, Arg-C and the like) every target peptide then has a decoy with the same start, length, mass and cleavage sites: also every protein N-terminal and Met-clipped one, so that peptide:clip_nterm_methionine and protein N-terminal modifications add the same number of target and decoy candidates (except peptides that equal their decoy, e.g. made of short segments or of K, R and P only; and modifications specific to a peptide's first residue, e.g. pyro-Glu on an N-terminal Q, apply to the target and its decoy alike only where that residue is kept in place). Semi- or non-specific digestion: as 'reverse'. Other enzymes: a warning names the decoys whose cleavage sites differ from the target's. No effect on decoys taken from the database (decoys 'auto' with decoys in it). The results differ from 'reverse'. (default: 'keep_termini') (valid: 'reverse', 'keep_termini')
  -Search:decoy_parity_report                               Diagnostic: log the candidate counts of the fragment index per label (target, decoy) and subclass (internal, protein N-terminal with and without initial M, Met-clipped; and their protein N-terminally acetylated occurrences, none with a fixed N-terminal modification such as TMT, which leaves no room for a variable one), and a checksum of generated decoys. Output unchanged.
  -Search:low_memory                                        Non-specific searches (peptide:enzyme_specificity=none; ignored otherwise): index the peptide masses, as the prefixes of one mother peptide per protein position, instead of the fragments of every peptidoform, and generate the fragments of the peptidoforms in the precursor window at query time. Finds the same candidates with a fraction of the memory (e.g. for immunopeptidomics against a human database), but takes more time per spectrum, the more the wider the precursor window.
  -Search:rescore <choice>                                  Rescore the PSMs of every search run (input file) in-process with Percolator (OpenMS' built-in implementation, as PercolatorAdapter uses by default) before the FDR filters: Percolator q-values become the main score (the SVM score and PEP are kept as meta values), Percolator rescores the top 'rescoring:top_hits' hits of each spectrum and only its best one per spectrum is kept, and FDR:PSM and FDR:protein are applied to the rescored PSMs. Needs decoys (see 'decoys') and at least 100 PSMs: a run without them, or for which Percolator fails, is not rescored and keeps its search engine scores, which the FDR filters then use (the ProSE tool warns). If that happens to some files of a multi-file search, the files rescored on their own keep their results, and the others get the scores of a model trained on the PSMs of those (on the scale of its training PSMs, with their PEP at the same score; q-values by target-decoy competition of each file's PSMs on that score); if Percolator can rescore no file on its own, all files are rescored together with one model. Only if that fails too, the files keep their search engine scores, and the pooled evaluation of the files (the ProSE tool's -out_merged) scores the proteins by ProSE's score 'ln(hyperscore)' of all PSMs instead of Percolator's PEP. See percolator:max_train_psms for the training PSMs. Set to 'false' to report the search engine scores. Keep it on for intermediates (FDR:protein 0) that ProteomicsLFQ finishes: it takes the PEP (meta value MS:1001493) as main score and stops for an input without PEPs. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:rescore_solver <choice>                           Solver of Percolator's SVMs with 'rescore' (Percolator's parameter 'solver'): 'newton' (default; explicit-Hessian Newton method with exact line search and warm starts: the same SVM optimum for a given training set as TRON, within the solver tolerance, much faster on large runs; as the earlier iterations of the cross-validation stop at a looser tolerance, they may choose slightly different training sets, so the models can differ from TRON's), 'tron' (Percolator's trust region Newton method, the solver of the percolator executable and of earlier ProSE versions) or 'lda' (Fisher's linear discriminant instead of the SVM: fastest, an approximation). (default: 'newton') (valid: 'newton', 'tron', 'lda')
  -Search:rescore_method <choice>                           With 'rescore': 'percolator' = Percolator's PSM-level FDR (default); 'reset' = Percolator-RESET (Freestone et al., J Proteome Res 2025): half of the decoy peptides (stems = unmodified sequences, by a random coin per stem) train the model, the other half estimates the FDR, which is controlled at the peptide (stem) level: every rescored hit gets the q-value of its stem as meta value 'RESET:peptide_q_value'. The main score (q-value) stays PSM-level, but under RESET it is a heuristic (not FDR-controlled): 2 (estimating decoy PSMs + 1) / target PSMs. Experimental. (default: 'percolator') (valid: 'percolator', 'reset')

Precursor (Parent Ion) Options. mass_tolerance_lower/_upper are positive magnitudes applied as [-lower, +upper] around the precursor mass.:
  -Search:precursor:mass_tolerance_lower <value>            Lower-side precursor-mass tolerance (positive magnitude; effective window is [-lower, +upper] around the precursor). When strongly asymmetric, also review precursor:isotope_error_min. (default: '10.0') (min: '0.0')
  -Search:precursor:mass_tolerance_upper <value>            Upper-side precursor-mass tolerance (positive magnitude). (default: '10.0') (min: '0.0')
  -Search:precursor:mass_tolerance_unit <choice>            Unit of precursor mass tolerance. (default: 'ppm') (valid: 'ppm', 'Da')
  -Search:precursor:min_charge <number>                     Minimum precursor charge to be considered. Spectra without a precursor charge are searched with every charge of the range; spectra whose recorded charge lies outside of it are searched at that charge, or not at all with precursor:recorded_charge_outside_range 'skip'. (default: '2') (min: '1' max: '20')
  -Search:precursor:max_charge <number>                     Maximum precursor charge to be considered (see precursor:min_charge). (default: '5') (min: '1' max: '20')
  -Search:precursor:recorded_charge_outside_range <choice>  A spectrum whose recorded precursor charge lies outside of precursor:min_charge..max_charge: 'skip' (the default, as OpenMS develop since #10713) does not search it; 'search' (the behaviour before #10713) searches it at its recorded charge. Spectra without a recorded charge are searched with every charge of the range. (default: 'skip') (valid: 'search', 'skip')
  -Search:precursor:isotope_error_min <number>              Minimum precursor isotope error searched, in 13C spacings (1.00336 Da) added to the observed precursor mass: -1 finds a peptide whose first 13C isotope peak was selected as the precursor. The PSM meta value 'isotope_error' reports the opposite sign by default (see report:isotope_error_convention). (default: '-1') (min: '-5' max: '5')
  -Search:precursor:isotope_error_max <number>              Maximum precursor isotope error searched, with the sign of precursor:isotope_error_min: +1 finds a peptide whose precursor was selected one 13C spacing below its monoisotopic peak. (default: '1') (min: '-5' max: '5')

Fragments (Product Ion) Options:
  -Search:fragment:mass_tolerance <value>                   Fragment mass tolerance (default: '20.0')
  -Search:fragment:mass_tolerance_unit <choice>             Unit of fragment m (default: 'ppm') (valid: 'ppm', 'Da')
  -Search:fragment:deisotope <choice>                       MS2 deisotoping (single-charge deconvolution) before searching. 'auto' deisotopes only when the fragment tolerance is within the high-resolution deisotoper range (<= 0.1 Da / <= 100 ppm) and skips it for low-resolution (e.g. ion-trap CID) data; 'true' always deisotopes (requires a high-resolution fragment tolerance); 'false' never deisotopes. (default: 'auto') (valid: 'auto', 'true', 'false')
  -Search:fragment:deisotope_min_peaks <number>             Minimum number of peaks, the monoisotopic peak included, of an isotope envelope that MS2 deisotoping collapses into its monoisotopic peak. With 2 (Sage-like), an ion whose M+2 peak is lost in the noise loses its M+1 peak too, so the M+1 peak no longer takes a place in the per-window peak quota, and a multiply charged ion seen with two isotope peaks is converted to charge 1; in dense spectra (e.g. timsTOF) more of the two-peak multiply charged envelopes are chance pairs. 3 is the earlier behaviour. (default: '2') (min: '2' max: '10')
  -Search:fragment:deisotope_charge_cap <choice>            Fragment charges that MS2 deisotoping tries. 'precursor' tries charges up to the precursor charge, but at most 3 (1-3 when the precursor charge is unknown): a fragment cannot carry more charges than its precursor, so a chance peak 1/3 Th above an ion of a 2+ precursor cannot turn the ion into a '3+' envelope at a wrong m/z. This is Sage-like; Sage caps at the precursor charge without the limit of 3 and counts an unknown charge as 3. 'none' tries charges 1-3 in every spectrum (the earlier behaviour). (default: 'precursor') (valid: 'precursor', 'none')
  -Search:fragment:deisotope_sum_intensity <choice>         Give the monoisotopic peak of each isotope envelope the summed intensity of the envelope (Sage-like). 'false' keeps the monoisotopic peak's own intensity (the earlier behaviour). (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:fragment:min_mz <number>                          Minimal fragment mz for database (default: '150') (min: '0')
  -Search:fragment:max_mz <number>                          Maximal fragment mz for database (default: '2000') (min: '0')
  -Search:fragment:min_ion_index <number>                   Ions with index less than or equal to this value are not added to the fragment index (use 0 to include all ions; 2 skips b1/b2/y1/y2). Low-index ions are often noisy and unreliable. (default: '2') (min: '0')
  -Search:fragment:query_spectrum <choice>                  Spectrum used for candidate retrieval. 'raw' queries the fragment index with all positive-intensity peaks, before deisotoping and local/top-N filtering, so that spectra whose matching fragments are not among the retained peaks still get candidates; it costs an additional peak list per spectrum and more fragment matches. 'processed' queries with the scoring peak list. 'auto' uses 'raw' for high-resolution fragments (<= 0.1 Da / <= 100 ppm) and 'processed' otherwise: at low resolution, the unfiltered peaks match many random fragments and crowd out the correct candidates. Scoring always uses the processed spectrum. (default: 'auto') (valid: 'auto', 'raw', 'processed')
  -Search:fragment:min_matched_ions <number>                Minimal number of matched ions to report a PSM (default: '5') (min: '0' max: '1000')
  -Search:fragment:max_charge <number>                      Max fragment charge (default: '2') (min: '1' max: '10')

Search:peaks:
  -Search:peaks:keep_n <number>                             Maximum number of MS2 peaks kept per spectrum (NLargest) before scoring. 0 = auto: a resolution-aware cap (~400 for high-res, ~80 at 0.5 Da, floor 60) that avoids the spurious low-resolution fragment matching which otherwise inflates HyperScore for targets and decoys alike. Set an explicit value to override (e.g. 400 for the legacy behavior). (default: '0') (min: '0')
  -Search:peaks:window_top <number>                         Maximum number of MS2 peaks kept per 100 Da window (WindowMower) before scoring. (default: '20') (min: '1')
  -Search:peaks:window_type <choice>                        Local peak filtering. 'auto' uses jump_full for high-resolution fragments (<= 0.1 Da / <= 100 ppm), preserving the strongest peaks even in a short final 100 Da window, and legacy jump filtering otherwise. 'jump' scales the last window's peak quota by its observed width; 'jump_full' keeps the full quota in every window. (default: 'auto') (valid: 'auto', 'jump', 'jump_full')

Modifications Options:
  -Search:modifications:fixed <list>                        Fixed modifications, specified using UniMod (www.unimod.org) terms, e.g. 'Carbamidomethyl (C)' (default: '[Carbamidomethyl (C)]') (valid:   -Search:modifications:variable <list>                     Variable modifications, specified using UniMod (www.unimod.org) terms, e.g. 'Oxidation (M)'. A terminus and a residue carry one modification each: a variable terminal modification (e.g. 'Acetyl (Protein N-term)', 'Gln->pyro-Glu (N-term Q)') is not searched where a fixed one sits on that terminus (e.g. 'TMT6plex (N-term)'), and a variable residue modification (e.g. 'Glutathione (C)') is not searched where a fixed one sits on that residue (e.g. 'Carbamidomethyl (C)').   -Search:modifications:variable_max_per_peptide <number>   Maximum number of residues carrying a variable modification per candidate peptide (default: '2') (min: '0' max: '5')

Annotation Options:
  -Search:annotate:PSM <list>                               Annotations added to each PSM. (default: '[ALL]') (valid: 'ALL', 'fragment_mz_error_median_ppm', 'precursor_mz_error_ppm', 'matched_prefix_ions_fraction', 'matched_suffix_ions_fraction', 'num_matched_peaks', 'matched_prefix_ions', 'matched_suffix_ions', 'longest_peptide_ion_sequence', 'matched_ion_current', 'fragment_annotation', 'hyperscore_zscore', 'ln_num_candidates', 'matched_ion_current_fraction', 'complementary_ions_fraction')
  -Search:annotate:self_trained_ion_priors <choice>         Learn per-run fragment ion likelihoods from the confident PSMs of each file and add the Percolator features of annotate:ion_prior_features to every hit. The spectra are split into two halves by scan parity. Each half trains a model on its rank-one target hits at target-decoy competition q <= annotate:ion_prior_train_fdr of the native score (their reversed sequences on the same spectra are the noise model), and every hit is scored by the model of the other half, so no PSM is scored by a model that saw its spectrum or its label. Needs annotate:ion_prior_min_psms training PSMs in each half, otherwise the features are 0. Not applied to target-only searches (decoys=ignore), which get no features. Native scores and candidate selection are unchanged. Independent of annotate:PSM. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_model <choice>                 Contexts and outcomes of the ion priors. 'rich': ion series, precursor charge, fragment charge, relative position, the residues at the cleavage site (bond before P, bond after D or E) and whether the complementary singly charged ion matched; outcome: intensity rank x mass error (|error| / tolerance below 0.25, below 0.5, up to 1). 'basic': ion series, precursor charge, fragment charge and relative position; outcome: intensity rank. (default: 'rich') (valid: 'rich', 'basic')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_peaks <choice>                 Peaks the ion priors rank and match. 'all': every peak after deisotoping, before the peaks:window_top and peaks:keep_n filters, so weak fragment ions count as evidence for the model without entering HyperScore. 'scored': the peaks that are scored. (default: 'all') (valid: 'all', 'scored')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_max_fragment_charge <number>   Highest fragment charge of the ions of the ion priors; capped at the precursor charge - 1 (at least 1). (default: '2') (min: '1')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_train_fdr <value>              Target-decoy competition q-value threshold selecting the training PSMs of annotate:self_trained_ion_priors. (default: '0.01') (min: '0.0' max: '1.0')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_min_psms <number>              Minimum number of training PSMs of annotate:self_trained_ion_priors in each half of the spectra; with fewer, the features are 0. (default: '100') (min: '1')
  -Search:annotate:ion_prior_features <list>                Features of annotate:self_trained_ion_priors written to every hit and listed for Percolator: ion_prior_llr (summed log-likelihood ratio of the ion outcomes, signal vs reversed noise), ion_prior_explained (share of the predicted ion presence that was observed), ion_prior_topk_observed (share of the 6 ions most likely present that were observed). (default: '[ion_prior_llr ion_prior_explained]') (valid: 'ion_prior_llr', 'ion_prior_explained', 'ion_prior_topk_observed')
  -Search:annotate:local_fragment_evidence <choice>         Add chance_match_surprise and mass_competition_evidence annotations and Percolator features. Uses local peak density and alternative intact/neutral-loss fragment assignments. Retains an additional peak list before window/top-N filtering, increasing memory use. Does not change native scores or candidate selection. Independent of annotate:PSM. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')

PeptDeep (ONNX) prediction-based rescoring features:
  -Search:peptdeep:enable                                   Add PeptDeep prediction-based rescoring features (ms2_cosine, ms2_spectral_angle, ms2_pearson, ms2_frac_pred_found, rt_abs_error) to every PSM. Uses the models shipped in OpenMS' 'share/OpenMS/models' unless 'peptdeep:ms2_model'/'peptdeep:rt_model' name others. Requires an OpenMS built with ONNX support.
  -Search:peptdeep:ms2_model <text>                         PeptDeep MS2 fragment-intensity ONNX model, used when 'peptdeep:enable' is true. A relative name is resolved against OpenMS' shared-data directory; an absolute path is used as given. (default: 'models/peptdeep_ms2_dynamic.onnx')
  -Search:peptdeep:rt_model <text>                          PeptDeep retention-time ONNX model. See 'peptdeep:ms2_model'. (default: 'models/peptdeep_rt_dynamic.onnx')
  -Search:peptdeep:instrument <choice>                      Instrument class passed to the PeptDeep MS2 model. An instrument that is not in this list is best approximated by the closest one that is. (default: 'QE') (valid: 'QE', 'Lumos', 'timsTOF', 'SciexTOF', 'ThermoTOF')
  -Search:peptdeep:nce <value>                              Normalised collision energy for the PeptDeep MS2 model. Negative selects it automatically from the collision energy recorded in the spectra, refined by scoring a small grid on confident PSMs. (default: '-1.0')
  -Search:peptdeep:rt_model_type <choice>                   Model mapping predicted onto observed retention time. (default: 'b_spline') (valid: 'b_spline', 'lowess', 'linear')
  -Search:peptdeep:batch_size <number>                      Peptides per ONNX inference call. The calls run side by side, one per thread: small ones keep the threads evenly busy and the memory low (on iPRG2015 with 16 threads, 64 took 40% less time than 500 and 1 GB less memory). (default: '64') (min: '1')

Peptide Options:
  -Search:peptide:min_size <number>                         Minimum size a peptide must have after digestion to be considered in the search. (default: '7') (min: '0')
  -Search:peptide:max_size <number>                         Maximum size a peptide must have after digestion to be considered in the search (0 = disabled). (default: '40') (min: '0')
  -Search:peptide:missed_cleavages <number>                 Number of missed cleavages. (default: '1') (min: '0')
  -Search:peptide:clip_nterm_methionine <choice>            Also search protein N-terminal peptides after removal of their initial methionine. The retained-M form is still searched; length, mass and missed-cleavage limits apply to each form. Set false for the previous search space. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:peptide:deduplicate <choice>                      Index each exact peptidoform once before candidate selection. Protein mappings still list every protein occurrence. Across database chunks, count each peptide/charge/isotope hypothesis once; retaining queried keys adds memory per spectrum. With low_memory, the repeated peptidoforms among the candidates of a spectrum are dropped instead. Set false for occurrence-based legacy candidates. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:peptide:protein_mapping <choice>                  How the hits are mapped to their proteins (peptide evidences, target_decoy, protein_references, protein hits). 'index': from the digest of the fragment index, which holds every protein occurrence of a candidate (with a protein-terminal modification: from a pass over the database for the hits' sequences); the result is that of PeptideIndexing, which runs instead wherever the index cannot reproduce it exactly (e.g. low_memory, a specificity other than full, a chunked database, symbols other than letters or long stretches of ambiguous residues in the database). 'PeptideIndexing': always search every hit in the whole database (Aho-Corasick), as before. (default: 'index') (valid: 'index', 'PeptideIndexing')
  -Search:peptide:enzyme_specificity <choice>               Enzyme cleavage specificity required for both peptide termini.
                                                              'full' : both termini must be enzyme-specific (canonical, e.g. tryptic).
                                                              'semi' : only one terminus needs to be enzyme-specific (semi-tryptic).
                                                              'none' : no enzyme constraint at either terminus; every substring of length
                                                                       [min_size, max_size] is enumerated. Use this for immunopeptidomics
                                                                       (e.g. HLA peptides 8..12mers). For very large search spaces consider
                                                                       tightening 'peptide:min_size'/'peptide:max_size'. (default: 'full') (valid: 'full', 'semi', 'none')
  -Search:peptide:motif <text>                              If set, only peptides whose unmodified sequence (one-letter codes) contains a match of this regular expression are searched, target and decoy peptides alike, e.g. 'N[^P][ST]' for N-glycosylation sequons or 'C' for cysteine-containing peptides; '^' and '$' anchor it to the peptide termini.
  -Search:peptide:min_mass <number>                         Minimal peptide mass for database (default: '100') (min: '0')
  -Search:peptide:max_mass <number>                         Maximal peptide mass for database (default: '9000') (min: '0')

Reporting Options:
  -Search:report:top_hits <number>                          Maximum number of top scoring hits per spectrum that are reported. With 'rescore', only the best hit per spectrum is reported after the rescoring, and the hits that Percolator rescores are set by 'rescoring:top_hits' (report:top_hits then applies to a run that is not rescored and to -out_pin). FDR:PSM > 0 and the protein FDR (FDR:protein > 0) also keep the best hit of each spectrum only, so values above 1 apply with 'rescore' false, FDR:PSM 0 and FDR:protein 0. (default: '1') (min: '1' max: '100')
  -Search:report:isotope_error_convention <choice>          Sign of the precursor isotope error reported for each PSM (meta value 'isotope_error', in 13C spacings of 1.00336 Da). 'observed_minus_theoretical': +1 means the observed precursor lies one 13C spacing above the peptide (its first 13C isotope peak was selected), as in MS-GF+, pepXML and Sage; PercolatorInfile and PercolatorAdapter (-out_pin, -percolator_executable) remove the offset from the precursor mass difference with this sign. The search parameters of the result record it as 'isotope_error_convention'. 'theoretical_minus_observed': the opposite sign, as written by earlier ProSE versions (no record in the search parameters); with it the Percolator input doubles the offset in dm/absdm instead of removing it. (default: 'observed_minus_theoretical') (valid: 'observed_minus_theoretical', 'theoretical_minus_observed')

Percolator rescoring ('rescore'):
  -Search:percolator:max_train_psms <number>                With 'rescore': the most PSMs Percolator trains a model on (0: all). Every PSM is scored, also beyond the cap. For a run with more PSMs, Percolator samples the training PSMs by spectrum (see 'rescoring:subset_mode'); for the model of the files of a multi-file search too small for a model of their own (trained on the files rescored on their own), each of these files contributes at most this many divided by the number of input files (reproducible random spectra), so that none dominates. The run log says when the training PSMs were sampled. Searches below the cap are not affected. Until all files of such a search are searched, ProSE keeps their PSMs before the rescoring for that model: all PSMs of the files too small, and of the others only their share (with 0, all PSMs of every file, also with the ProSE tool's -out_pin). (default: '500000') (min: '0')

Options of the Percolator training of the rescoring (see 'rescore'):
  -Search:rescoring:top_hits <number>                       With 'rescore': the top scoring hits per spectrum (by the search engine score) that Percolator rescores: its training and scoring rows, of which its target-decoy competition keeps the best per spectrum, which is reported. 2 (default) gives Percolator the runner-up of each spectrum as well; more hits cost time and memory. 0 = 'report:top_hits' (as earlier ProSE versions, whose Percolator rescored the reported hits). The search keeps these hits for Percolator (with -out_pin also the reported ones): a run that Percolator does not rescore (fewer than 100 PSMs, no decoy PSM, a failure) reports at most rescoring:top_hits (if above 0) of its report:top_hits hits per spectrum; with decoys 'ignore' (no rescoring) report:top_hits. (default: '2') (min: '0')
  -Search:rescoring:subset_mode <choice>                    With a training cap ('percolator:max_train_psms' > 0) on a run above it (Percolator's parameter 'subset_mode'): 'out_of_fold' (default) assigns every spectrum to one of Percolator's three cross-validation folds first, draws the training sample (whole spectra, at random from the entire run, seeded), trains each fold's model on the sampled spectra of the other folds only, and scores every PSM with the model of its own fold, which never saw its spectrum. 'out_of_fold_avg': the same, but the PSMs of the spectra outside the sample (which no model saw) are scored by the average of the fold models. 'in_sample' = the percolator executable's --subset-max-train: cross-validation on the sample and all PSMs scored by the average of the fold models (the sampled PSMs by two models that trained on them). 'learning_curve' (also without a cap): out-of-fold training on nested samples of 'rescoring:curve_start', twice, four times ... as many PSMs, up to 'percolator:max_train_psms' (0: 1,000,000) or the whole run but the validation spectra (the end of the random order, at most 250,000 PSMs or 20 %); each fold's model stops at the first doubling that adds less than 'rescoring:curve_min_gain' or 2 standard errors of targets at 1 % among the validation spectra of the other folds (no label of its own fold is used), and is not trained further; every PSM is scored by its fold's model. The sizes and the curve are recorded as Percolator:subset_curve. (default: 'out_of_fold') (valid: 'out_of_fold', 'out_of_fold_avg', 'in_sample', 'learning_curve')
  -Search:rescoring:curve_start <number>                    'rescoring:subset_mode' learning_curve: PSMs of the first sample. (default: '100000') (min: '1')
  -Search:rescoring:curve_min_gain <value>                  'rescoring:subset_mode' learning_curve: a fold stops at the first doubling that adds less than this fraction of validation targets. (default: '2.0e-03') (min: '0.0')
  -Search:rescoring:iterations <number>                     Iterations of Percolator's cross-validation training (the percolator executable's --maxiter). Fewer are faster; the results differ. (default: '10') (min: '1')
  -Search:rescoring:tron_epsilon <value>                    Stopping tolerance of the SVM solver (TRON) of Percolator's training (Percolator: 1e-7). Larger values (e.g. 1e-3) train much faster on large runs; the results differ. (default: '1.0e-07') (min: '1.0e-15')
  -Search:rescoring:warm_start                              Each SVM training of an iteration starts from the weights of the previous iteration instead of 0 (faster; the results differ).
  -Search:rescoring:single_cneg                             Percolator trains with one Cneg (3 x the target/decoy ratio x Cpos) instead of selecting among three by cross-validation (faster; the results differ).
  -Search:rescoring:use_pi0 <choice>                        Percolator's q-values and PEPs with its pi0 estimate (default) or with pi0 = 1, as the percolator executable computes them with target-decoy competition (-Y). 'false' gives slightly fewer PSMs and a lower measured false discovery proportion; the results differ. 'auto' decides as the executable (OpenMS develop's in-process default since #10721): with ProSE's target-decoy competition that is 'false'. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false', 'auto')
  -Search:rescoring:calc_mass_feature                       Percolator's training uses the CalcMass column (the theoretical m/z) as a feature. 'false' (the default): as the percolator executable, which reads it as metadata (OpenMS develop since #10717); 'true': the behaviour before #10717. The results differ.
  -Search:rescoring:fixed_enzyme_termini                    A protein terminus counts as an enzymatic site for the enzN/enzC features of the rescoring and of '-out_pin' (Percolator's convention). Default 'false' keeps the former features, in which a peptide at a protein N-terminus has enzN = 0 and one at a protein C-terminus that does not end in a cleavage residue has enzC = 0; the results differ. The peptide after an initial Met (the Met-clipped protein N-terminal peptide of peptide:clip_nterm_methionine) counts as N-terminal too. Experimental: tested only with decoy_method 'keep_termini'; without the Met-clip rule, with the decoys of a database of reversed proteins (which rarely start with M) enzN = 0 was left almost only to Met-clipped peptides of targets and the FDR was no longer controlled (entrapment FDP 13-24 % at 1 % q).
  -Search:rescoring:terminal_features <choice>              Two more rescoring features: 'protein_nterm' (1 if the peptide occurs at a protein N-terminus) and 'met_clipped' (1 if it occurs only as the Met-clipped protein N-terminal peptide of peptide:clip_nterm_methionine). They let the rescoring learn the different rate of correct matches of these small peptide classes. Use them only with decoys that populate both classes like the targets (decoy_method 'keep_termini'): with decoys that rarely start with M (reversed proteins, reused with decoys 'auto') they would tell targets from decoys, so they are switched off with a warning. The results differ. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:rescoring:stratum_q_values <choice>               Also q-values per stratum (meta values 'stratum' and 'stratum_q_value'): the rescored PSMs of protein N-terminal peptides (incl. Met-clipped ones) and the others each get target-decoy competition of their own ((decoys + 1) / targets, as mokapot's grouped confidence). For a list of N-terminal peptides at an FDR, use the stratum q-value; the main score stays the pooled q-value. Needs decoys that populate the N-terminal classes like the targets (decoy_method 'keep_termini'; with reused decoys that rarely start with M it is switched off with a warning). With rescore_method 'reset' the values are computed on RESET's scores (both decoy sets count as decoys with reset:decoys 'two'), not as RESET's estimate. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:rescoring:peptide_level <choice>                  Also Percolator's peptide-level q-values (default; 'false' leaves them out), as the percolator executable reports peptides (without -U): after the target-decoy competition of the spectra, the best PSM of each peptide (modified sequence) and label competes, and q-values and PEPs are estimated on these peptides. Every rescored hit gets the values of its peptide as meta values 'percolator_peptide_q_value' and 'percolator_peptide_pep'; a list of peptides at an FDR is the target peptides with percolator_peptide_q_value at most that FDR (the distinct peptides of the PSMs at an FDR are not FDR-controlled at the peptide level). The main score stays the PSM-level q-value. Default Percolator rescoring only (not with rescore_method 'reset', which reports RESET:peptide_q_value, nor with rescore_reuse models). (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')

Percolator-RESET options (rescore_method 'reset'):
  -Search:reset:salt <text>                                 Rescore_method 'reset': salt of the decoy coins (unsigned 64-bit integer); empty = random per run. The salt is recorded in the search parameters ('RESET:salt'). Never pick the best of several salts.
  -Search:reset:learner <choice>                            Rescore_method 'reset': 'svm' = linear SVM; 'mlp' = small MLP ensemble (experimental). (default: 'svm') (valid: 'svm', 'mlp')
  -Search:reset:mlp_members <number>                        Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp': networks per ensemble. (default: '8') (min: '1')
  -Search:reset:mlp_hidden <number>                         Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp': units of each of the two hidden layers. (default: '16') (min: '1')
  -Search:reset:mlp_epochs <number>                         Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp': training epochs of the first round. (default: '30') (min: '1')
  -Search:reset:mlp_warm_epochs <number>                    Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp': > 0 = the networks continue from the previous round for this many epochs (warm starts); 0 = trained afresh (mlp_epochs) every round. (default: '3') (min: '0')
  -Search:reset:mlp_crossfit <choice>                       Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp': score each peptide by the networks that did not train on it (3 folds by sequence hash); 'false' = one final ensemble (memorizes its training decoys: fewer peptides). (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:reset:mlp_norm_min_decoys <number>                Rescore_method 'reset', learner 'mlp', cross-fitted: normalize the folds' scores (as Percolator) only if every fold has at least this many training decoys (0 = always). (default: '1000') (min: '0')
  -Search:reset:representative <choice>                     Rescore_method 'reset': the PSM that represents a peptide (stem): 'model' (default) = after each training round, best score under the new model (+2 % peptides on DIA; the FDR then also assumes that null targets and decoys are exchangeable over their whole PSM collections, including their sizes); 'search' = best search engine score (the paper). (default: 'model') (valid: 'search', 'model')
  -Search:reset:iterations <number>                         Rescore_method 'reset': training rounds (the paper: 5; on DIA pseudo-spectra 5 rounds stop far from convergence: 82 % of the peptides of 20 rounds on HeLa). (default: '25') (min: '1')
  -Search:reset:audit                                       Rescore_method 'reset': feature-symmetry audit before training (log).
  -Search:reset:decoys <choice>                             Rescore_method 'reset': 'single' = one decoy database (default); 'two' = the two-decoy mode of Percolator-RESET: the database holds a second decoy set of the same construction (accessions starting with reset:second_decoy_prefix, also recognized as decoys by the decoy marker), the spectra are searched against targets and both decoy sets, and RESET estimates the FDR with the factors of two decoy databases (fewer peptides lost to the decoy split, less variation between salts; the search is larger). (default: 'single') (valid: 'single', 'two')
  -Search:reset:second_decoy_prefix <text>                  Rescore_method 'reset', reset:decoys 'two': accession prefix of the proteins of the second decoy set. (default: 'DECOY_X_')
  -Search:reset:stem_table <text>                           Rescore_method 'reset': write the stems with their q-values (tab-separated) to this file, or, if it ends with '/' or is a directory, to <dir>/<spectra file base name>.reset_stems.tsv per run.

Percolator model reuse across the files of a search ('rescore'):
  -Search:rescore_reuse:mode <choice>                       Which PSMs train the Percolator model of a run ('rescore'). 'per_file': the run itself, with Percolator's three-fold cross-validation. The other modes cross-fit: the spectra are split into two halves at random (by Percolator's seed), one model is trained per half on about 'rescore_reuse:max_train' PSMs, and every PSM is scored by the model of the other half, so that no PSM is scored by a model that was trained on it (5 % of the spectra of each half are not trained on: their decoys put the scores of their own half's model on one scale; they are scored by the other model). 'joint' (a multi-file search): the training PSMs are drawn from all files, the same number from each, and the files are rescored once all are searched (mokapot's joint model). 'static' (a multi-file search): they are drawn from the first 'rescore_reuse:static_files' files, and every later file is rescored with these models after its search, unless the features of its PSMs differ from those of the training PSMs by more than 'rescore_reuse:max_shift': then it is rescored as with 'per_file'. 'own': every file trains its two models on its own PSMs. In every mode the q-values and PEPs of a file come from its own target-decoy competition (Percolator's, as with 'per_file'). 'joint' and 'static' need an unchunked closed search of several files and keep all files' PSMs in memory until they are rescored; otherwise 'per_file' applies. (default: 'per_file') (valid: 'per_file', 'joint', 'static', 'own')
  -Search:rescore_reuse:max_train <number>                  Training PSMs per model of 'rescore_reuse:mode' (0: all PSMs of the half but the calibration spectra). The spectra are drawn at random, all PSMs of a drawn spectrum together, so the number is met on average; the files share it equally (a file with fewer PSMs gives all). (default: '250000') (min: '0')
  -Search:rescore_reuse:static_files <number>               With 'rescore_reuse:mode' static: the number of (first) input files that train the models. (default: '1') (min: '1')
  -Search:rescore_reuse:max_shift <value>                   With 'rescore_reuse:mode' static: the largest domain shift for which a file is rescored with the static models. The shift is max |2 AUC - 1| over the features the models use, AUC comparing the file's PSMs with the training PSMs, targets and decoys alike (0: same distribution, 1: separated). (default: '0.5') (min: '0.0' max: '1.0')

False Discovery Rate control (requires decoys):
  -Search:FDR:PSM <value>                                   Filter PSMs based on q-value (e.g., 0.05 = 5% FDR; 0 disables the filter). Target and decoy PSMs are filtered alike by the threshold; decoys that pass are kept (no decoy-specific stripping here — decoys are removed only at protein-FDR finalization). With 'rescore' (the default), the threshold applies to the Percolator q-values, the main score of the rescored PSMs. Without rescoring ('rescore' false, or a run that Percolator did not rescore), 0 computes no q-values: every PSM keeps its search engine score ('ln(hyperscore)') as main score, e.g. as input for Percolator or a downstream FDR step. Above 0, the target-decoy q-value (see FDR:PSM_groups) replaces the main score (the search engine score is kept as meta value 'ln(hyperscore)_score') and only the best hit of each spectrum is kept. If no PSM matched a decoy, the q-value of the T PSMs is the conservative 1/T (target-decoy competition with +1), so a lower threshold reports no PSM instead of lifting the filter. Needs decoys ('decoys' auto or generate); with 'ignore', a threshold above 0 is skipped with a warning (the QPX run view of the ProSE tool's -qpx_extended records per input whether the filter was applied: 'psm_fdr_applied'). Afterwards, the proteins that no remaining PSM references are removed. The final PSM FDR with FDR:protein > 0 (e.g. 0.01), a loose evidence threshold of the intermediates with FDR:protein 0 (e.g. 0.1; see there). (default: '0.0') (min: '0.0' max: '1.0')
  -Search:FDR:PSM_groups <choice>                           Target-decoy competitions of FDR:PSM. 'pooled' ranks all PSMs together. 'scored_charges' computes q-values separately for PSMs scored with different numbers of fragment charges (scoring:fragment_charges): HyperScore grows with the number of theoretical ions, so when multiply charged fragments are scored for precursors of charge 3 and above but not for charge 2, a pooled competition is dominated by the higher precursor charges. With singly charged fragments only (deisotoped, high-resolution spectra) there is one group, as with 'pooled'. With fragment:max_charge above 2, precursor charges of 4 and above form further groups, which accept few PSMs per run (tens), so their q-values (D/T, as FalseDiscoveryRate) are coarse. A group without decoy or without target PSMs falls back to 'pooled'. (default: 'scored_charges') (valid: 'scored_charges', 'pooled')
  -Search:FDR:protein <value>                               Picked-protein FDR threshold (e.g. 0.01 = 1 %), and the switch between the two modes of a search. Above 0, the search is the whole experiment: its proteins are inferred on the pooled PSMs of all its inputs, filtered at this protein FDR, and the PSMs filtered finally, so every output is the final result (the decoys removed, see the ProSE tool's -keep_decoys). With 0 (the default), the search is one part of a larger experiment, e.g. one shard of files searched on one compute node: every output is a per-run intermediate (PSM q-values and PEPs per run, FDR:PSM as a loose evidence threshold, decoys and the proteins the search indexed for the run's PSMs kept), to be finished downstream on all parts together (e.g. ProteomicsLFQ, or IDMerger, ProteinInference or Epifany, FalseDiscoveryRate and IDFilter). Uses the picked-protein approach (Savitski et al. 2015) which pairs target and decoy proteins by accession. The protein inference and the picked-protein FDR use the PSMs up to FDR:protein_inference_PSM, and a protein scores with the best posterior error probability of its PSMs (Percolator's, with 'rescore'; without it the best search engine score). The proteins and the protein groups (which have q-values of their own) are filtered at the threshold, and only the proteins of the groups that pass are kept. Proteins without a reported PSM (one that passes FDR:PSM) are removed afterwards, and so are the PSMs all of whose proteins failed the protein FDR. Needs decoys ('decoys' auto or generate). (default: '0.0') (min: '0.0' max: '1.0')
  -Search:FDR:protein_inference_PSM <value>                 Q-value cutoff of the PSMs that protein inference and the protein FDR (FDR:protein > 0) use; FDR:PSM still filters the reported PSMs. Without the protein FDR it has no effect. The q-values are those FDR:PSM filters on (Percolator's with 'rescore', else see FDR:PSM_groups). A looser cutoff than FDR:PSM leaves more decoy proteins for the picked-protein FDR, as ProteomicsLFQ infers proteins on PSMs at a looser q-value than its PSM FDR. Values at or below FDR:PSM use the PSMs of FDR:PSM; with FDR:PSM 0 (no PSM filter) all PSMs are used. (default: '0.1') (min: '0.0' max: '1.0')

Search/Scoring Limits:
  -Search:scoring:max_candidates_per_spectrum <number>      The number of initial hits for which we calculate a score (default: '50') (min: '1')
  -Search:scoring:fragment_charges <choice>                 Fragment charges of the theoretical spectra that score each candidate. 'single' scores singly charged fragments only. 'multiple' adds charges up to min(precursor charge - 1, fragment:max_charge). 'auto' uses 'multiple' when the MS2 spectra are not deisotoped (fragment:deisotope=false, or a fragment tolerance above the deisotoper's limit of 0.1 Da / 100 ppm, as for ion-trap CID) and 'single' otherwise, because deisotoping converts fragments to charge 1. (default: 'auto') (valid: 'auto', 'single', 'multiple')
  -Search:scoring:method <choice>                           Native score. 'hyperscore' is the X!Tandem HyperScore. 'mass_accuracy' weights each matched fragment's contribution to HyperScore's ion counts and intensity product by exp(-0.5 * (error / scoring:mass_error_sd)^2), with the error in ppm, so that matches near the theoretical m/z count fully and matches at the edge of the tolerance count little; exact matches score as HyperScore. The kernel is centred at 0 and suits high-resolution fragments only. 'auto' (default) uses 'mass_accuracy' for fragment tolerances in ppm within the high-resolution range (<= 100 ppm) and 'hyperscore' otherwise (Da tolerances, e.g. ion-trap CID). Matching tolerances, candidates and the unweighted match annotations are unchanged. The score type stays 'ln(hyperscore)'; the search parameters record scoring:method_resolved when the weighted score is used. (default: 'auto') (valid: 'hyperscore', 'mass_accuracy', 'auto')
  -Search:scoring:mass_error_sd <value>                     Standard deviation in ppm of the Gaussian fragment mass-error kernel of scoring:method 'mass_accuracy'. Fixed (not fitted) and independent of the matching tolerance and of calibration. (default: '7.0') (min: '1.0e-06')

Theoretical ion series toggles:
  -Search:ions:add_y_ions <choice>                          Add peaks of y-ions to the spectrum (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:ions:add_b_ions <choice>                          Add peaks of b-ions to the spectrum (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')
  -Search:ions:add_a_ions                                   Add peaks of a-ions to the spectrum
  -Search:ions:add_c_ions                                   Add peaks of c-ions to the spectrum
  -Search:ions:add_x_ions                                   Add peaks of  x-ions to the spectrum
  -Search:ions:add_z_ions                                   Add peaks of z-ions (y - NH3) to the spectrum. These are not the z+1 ions of ETD-type spectra; see ions:by_activation and ions:add_zp1_ions.
  -Search:ions:add_zp1_ions                                 Add peaks of z+1 ions (z-dot, y - NH2) to the spectrum, the main C-terminal fragments of ETD, EThcD and ETciD spectra, for all spectra. ions:by_activation adds them (with c ions) for spectra recorded as electron-activated; set this, typically with ions:add_c_ions, for ETD-type data without activation information.
  -Search:ions:by_activation <choice>                       Score spectra whose precursor was activated by electrons (ETD, ECD, EThcD or ETciD, as recorded in the input file) with c and z+1 ions in addition to the ion series above. Other spectra, and spectra without activation information, use the ion series above. (default: 'true') (valid: 'true', 'false')

Automatic mass accuracy calibration (two-pass search). A fast first pass on a subset of spectra estimates instrument-specific mass accuracy, then the main search uses the calibrated (typically tighter) tolerances for better discrimination.:
  -Search:calibration:enabled                               If enabled, run a fast calibration pass on a subset of spectra before the main search. Estimates tighter precursor and fragment tolerances from confident PSMs. The fragment index is NOT rebuilt — only query-time tolerances are tightened. Inspired by MSFragger's calibrate_mass and OpenNuXL's autotune.
  -Search:calibration:subset_ratio <value>                  Fraction of spectra (by TIC, highest first) used for the calibration pass (0.0-1.0). (default: '0.1') (min: '0.01' max: '1.0')
  -Search:calibration:min_psms <number>                     Minimum number of confident PSMs required for calibration. If fewer are found, calibration is skipped and the user-configured tolerances are used. (default: '50') (min: '1')

Search:database:
  -Search:database:chunk_size <number>                      Split the protein database into chunks of at most this many proteins for fragment index building. 0 = disabled (load entire database at once). Enable for very large databases (e.g. MHC-II immunopeptidomics with variants) that exceed available memory. Each chunk builds its own fragment index; results are merged across chunks before post-processing. Calibration (when enabled) runs on a strided, size-bounded sample of the full decoy-augmented database — sample size is tied to chunk_size so calibration memory respects the same budget as main-search chunks. In multi-file mode (-in a.mzML b.mzML), the chunk-major path builds each chunk's fragment index once and scores all files against it before moving to the next chunk. Note that chunk_size bounds only the fragment-index memory: the chunk-major schedule holds ALL input files' preprocessed MS2 spectra in memory for the whole search, so budget for their sum as well, or split very large cohorts across separate ProSE invocations. (default: '0') (min: '0')

Search of precursors co-isolated with the selected one (chimeric spectra).:
  -Search:chimeric:enabled                                  Also identify precursors co-isolated with the selected one (after the andes chimeric cascade). The survey scan of each MS2 spectrum is searched for isotope envelopes in its isolation window that fit averagine (needs centroided MS1 spectra in the input and isolation windows on the precursors). After the search, the peaks the best hit of the spectrum explains are removed, and the rest is searched for each co-isolated precursor in turn, at its m/z and charge; the best hit of each removes the peaks it explains before the next is searched. The best hit's peptide is not a hit of a co-isolated precursor, and a co-isolated precursor whose best hit repeats a PSM of the spectrum (same peptide and charge) is dropped. These PSMs get the spectrum reference of their spectrum and the PSM meta value 'chimeric_rank' 2 (the others 1). Not with database:chunk_size > 0.
  -Search:chimeric:max_coisolated <number>                  At most this many co-isolated precursors per spectrum, the most intense first. (default: '4') (min: '1')
  -Search:chimeric:max_kl <value>                           Largest Kullback-Leibler divergence of an isotope envelope in the survey scan from averagine (first four isotope peaks) to accept it as a co-isolated precursor. (default: '0.3') (min: '0.0')
  -Search:chimeric:top_hits <number>                        At most this many hits of a co-isolated precursor are reported (and at most report:top_hits). Each takes about as much memory as a hit of a spectrum. (default: '3') (min: '1')
  -Search:chimeric:mz_tolerance <value>                     Tolerance (ppm) for the m/z of the isotope peaks in the survey scan. (default: '10.0') (min: '0.0')

                                                            
Common TOPP options:
  -ini <file>                                               Use the given TOPP INI file
  -log <file>                                               Name of log file (created only when specified)
  -debug <n>                                                Sets the debug level (default: '0')
  -threads <n>                                              Sets the number of threads allowed to be used by the TOPP tool (0 = all available cores) (default: '1')
  -write_ini <file>                                         Writes the default configuration file
  -write_ctd <out_dir>                                      Writes the common tool description file(s) (Toolname(s).ctd) to <out_dir>
  -write_nested_cwl <out_dir>                               Writes the Common Workflow Language file(s) (Toolname(s).cwl) to <out_dir>
  -write_cwl <out_dir>                                      Writes the Common Workflow Language file(s) (Toolname(s).cwl) to <out_dir>, but enforce a flat parameter hierarchy
  -write_nested_json <out_dir>                              Writes the default configuration file
  -write_json <out_dir>                                     Writes the default configuration file, but compatible to the flat hierarchy
  -no_progress                                              Disables progress logging to command line
  -force                                                    Overrides tool-specific checks
  -test                                                     Enables the test mode (needed for internal use only)
  --help                                                    Shows options
  --helphelp                                                Shows all options (including advanced)
